FIRMABLE · RESEARCH EXTENSION

Learning from unfamiliar sources

Three government datasets. Agent-authored mappings. An evolving, versioned ontology.

Experimental candidates · Human review pending · Original submission unchanged

How the system works

01 · Source intake

Research → licence → download → snapshot hash

02 · Inspector agent

Actual bytes → reader, row grain and nested roles

03 · Freeze evidence

Discovery / validation / sealed test partitions

04 · Architect agent

Reuse concepts or propose typed, scoped additions

05 · Mapper agent

Generate an executable config and unmapped reasons

06 · Validate + critique

Run shared engine; Critic challenges meaning; five rounds per pass, with logged resumes

07 · Test + registry

Sealed test → immutable candidate → verified offline replay

08 · Profiles + review

Generic facets and ABN link proposals; human approval before promotion

The four named agents are separate model calls using the same model. Source research was assistant-led; validation, extraction, registry management and projection are deterministic code.

Type/constraint errors are not semantic mapping errors. A zero value here does not imply perfect precision or complete coverage. Samples include forced preview rows and are not population estimates.

Historical Australian Government Contract Notice Data

Government customer relationships, contract scope, procurement category, value and dates. One published-date interval; not all contracts.

Downloaded source · Publisher documentation · Creative Commons Attribution 3.0 Australia

Publisher: Department of Finance. Retrieved 2026-11-22T22:37:19+00:00. Snapshot 67cd3dbaaba8b21a704180afa86bb6f3bd0f30f9644ecb032a318affd9dad09c

New ontology concepts

ConceptOwnerTypeMeaning
dyn.contract.notice_numbercontractstringThe publisher's contract notice identifier as published in contracts[0].id (e.g. CN4280221). It identifies the notice within the publisher's system; it is not a supplier key, ABN or ACN. It is not unique per release: an amendment release (tag 'contractAmendment') restates the original notice id, so the contract role key contracts[0].id collides across the original and amendment releases and the amendment release is not distinguishable by the contract key alone. The engine's named-role identity may repeat across records, so this is a bounded identity limitation, not an overwrite.
dyn.contract.signed_atcontractdateThe calendar date on which the contract was signed as stated in the notice (contracts[0].dateSigned). The source states a time of day, but this concept is a date and the time is not preserved; it is not the contract start date, the notice publication date, or the business's registration date.
dyn.contract.start_datecontractdateThe first day of the contract period stated in the notice (contracts[0].period.startDate). The source states a full timestamp whose time component is not constant across rows (e.g. 14:00Z on some rows, 13:00Z on others), so no fixed local-midnight convention can be assumed. The parse_date transform truncates the time, so the emitted value is the UTC date component of the stated timestamp; the local contract day may differ by one. It is not the release publication date and not the moment the notice was observed.
dyn.contract.end_datecontractdateThe last day of the contract period stated in the notice (contracts[0].period.endDate), i.e. the planned end of this contract's term. The source states a full timestamp whose time component is not constant across rows (e.g. 13:00Z observed), so no fixed local-midnight convention can be assumed. The parse_date transform truncates the time, so the emitted value is the UTC date component of the stated timestamp; the local contract day may differ by one. It is not a licence expiry and not a business's cessation.
dyn.contract.descriptioncontractstringThe scope of work as the procuring entity described it in the notice (contracts[0].description), e.g. 'Stakeholder Engagement and Consultation Services'. It is source-stated activity text and describes this procurement, not the supplier's company-wide industry or sector.
dyn.contract.valuecontractdecimalThe total maximum value of the contract over its life as reported in the notice (contracts[0].value.amount), qualified by the ISO 4217 currency in contracts[0].value.currency. It is not annual expenditure, not revenue or business income, and the contract term is captured by dyn.contract.start_date and dyn.contract.end_date on this same entity.
dyn.contract.statuscontractenumThe status of the contract as reported in the notice at extraction time (contracts[0].status), expressed with the OCDS contract status codelist and 'unknown' for anything not published. Only 'active' is observed in this snapshot; the other values are codelist-derived, not observed. It reflects the notice data, is not a guarantee of the contract's current status, and is not the supplier's registration status.
dyn.contract.item_classification_codecontractstringThe classification code of a contracted item as published (contracts[0].items[0].classification.id), always paired with the publisher's scheme (contracts[0].items[0].classification.scheme, here UNSPSC). The code describes the goods or services bought under this contract, not the supplier's company-wide industry. Only the first item of a contract is read at this grain; additional items are not captured.
dyn.contract.procurement_methodcontractenumHow the contract was let as reported in the notice (tender.procurementMethod), expressed with the OCDS procurement method codelist and 'unknown' if not published. It describes the procurement process only and says nothing about contract size, supplier capability or the notice population. On an amendment release the value is as restated in that release and may not differ from the original.
dyn.contract.limited_tender_exemptcontractenumWhether the notice marks the limited tender as exempt (tender.limitedTenderExempt), published as 1 or 0 and mapped to true/false; a blank value is 'unknown', never false. Both 'true' (from raw '1') and 'false' (from raw '0') are observed in this snapshot; 'unknown' is codelist-derived, not observed. It records a stated procurement exemption, not a judgement about whether the exemption was justified.
dyn.contract.limited_tender_reason_codecontractstringThe exemption clause code the publisher gives for a limited tender (tender.limitedTenderReasonCode, e.g. TR, AD), kept as stated. It refers to a Commonwealth procurement exemption clause; the separate verbatim reason text is captured by dyn.contract.limited_tender_reason and is not assumed to quote this code. Observed on one row only in this snapshot; the mapping is verified on that row (emitted value 'TR', no value errors), but coverage is limited to that single observation.
dyn.contract.limited_tender_reasoncontractstringThe publisher's full wording of the reason a limited tender was used (tender.limitedTenderReason), kept verbatim as source evidence with its clause numbering and headings. It is a reported justification, not an assessment made by us. Observed on one row only in this snapshot; the mapping is verified on that row (verbatim reason text emitted, no value errors), but coverage is limited to that single observation.

Reuse and explicit omissions

Existing conceptJustification
entity.legal_nameOCDS parties[].name is the single party name the publisher states for the supplier and for the procuring entity. The publisher does not distinguish registered from trading names, so the value is recorded as a published party name with no current_legal_name claim_scope; no legal form is inferred from forms such as 'The Trustee for ... Trust' or 'Data#3 Limited'.
entity.abnparties[].additionalIdentifiers[] where scheme is AU-ABN carries an 11-digit ABN for both the supplier party and the procuring entity party; entity.abn already exists and requires checksum validation rather than length checks. An ABN is an identifier only and proves nothing about legal form.
address.localityparties[].address.locality is the suburb or locality of the party's own stated address, which is exactly what address.locality holds for a business.
address.stateparties[].address.region values are Australian state and territory abbreviations (NT, QLD, VIC, ACT) matching the address.state enum. Non-Australian regions such as 'Outside Australia' are coerced to OTHER, which conflates all non-Australian regions and loses the raw text; the emitted OTHER means 'not an Australian state/territory', not a location, and must not be read as an Australian state. ext.entity.operating_region is not used because a stated address region is not a claimed service footprint.
address.postcodeparties[].address.postalCode is the postal code of the party's stated address, matching address.postcode. Foreign postcodes (e.g. '96960', '22102-1036') are kept as written and are not asserted to be Australian postcodes.
address.countryparties[].address.countryName is the country of the party's stated address as written (AUSTRALIA, COLOMBIA, SWITZERLAND, UNITED ARAB EMIRATES), which is what address.country stores.
observation.source_record_idreleases[].id identifies exactly one published release, which is the row grain, so it is the natural traceable record key for observation.source_record_id.
observation.observed_atreleases[].date is the publication timestamp of the notice, i.e. when the source stated this record; it is not valid_from and not the contract start date. The parse_date transform truncates the time of day, a documented precision loss.
observation.source_idThe catalogue metadata gives a stable dataset identity (austender_new) for these notices, which is what observation.source_id is for.
observation.licenceThe dataset metadata declares the CC-BY 3.0 Australia licence; observation.licence records the licence id 'cc-by' from the metadata, not the full licence_detail text.
ext.contact.role_emailReused only for the procuring entity party's contactPoint.email, which publishes a role mailbox (tenders@finance.gov.au). It is not reused for supplier parties, whose contactPoint carries the buyer's boilerplate.
ext.contact.phoneReused only for the procuring entity party's contactPoint.telephone, where published. It is not reused for supplier parties, whose contactPoint telephone is the buyer's boilerplate.
Columns deliberately left unmapped
ColumnReason
contracts[0].amendments[0].idAmendment fields ARE present on at least one amendment release (held-out ordinal 19, tag 'contractAmendment', contracts[0].amendments[0].id = 'CN4280171-A1'), so the earlier 'not present in the inspected snapshot' reason was inaccurate. Left unmapped because (a) only a single amendment observation exists, (b) there is no amendment entity type in the ontology, and (c) the contract key contracts[0].id collides across the original and amendment releases as already disclosed in dyn.contract.notice_number. No amendment concept is proposed from a single held-out row.
contracts[0].amendments[0].amendsReleaseIDObserved on at least one amendment release (held-out ordinal 19, tag 'contractAmendment', value 'prod-2416a68113334cca9867238cb761658c-8ea45a4cba6b302f9a4c4cc4babc2319'), so the earlier 'not present in the inspected snapshot' reason was inaccurate. Left unmapped because only one observation exists, there is no amendment entity type, and the contract key contracts[0].id collides across original and amendment releases as disclosed in dyn.contract.notice_number. No amendment-link concept is proposed from a single held-out row.
contracts[0].amendments[0].releaseIDObserved on at least one amendment release (held-out ordinal 19, tag 'contractAmendment', value 'prod-2416a68113334cca9867238cb761658c-927300ea26db3e1e8fb91d355fbcc4fe'), so the earlier 'not present in the inspected snapshot' reason was inaccurate. Left unmapped because only one observation exists, there is no amendment entity type, and the contract key contracts[0].id collides across original and amendment releases as disclosed in dyn.contract.notice_number. No amendment concept is proposed from a single held-out row.
supplier_party.rolesroles is the discriminator used to bind supplier_party and procuring_entity_party; its meaning is already carried by the CONTRACT_AWARDED_TO and CONTRACT_PROCURED_BY relationships, so a separate role claim would duplicate them.
procuring_entity_party.rolesroles is the discriminator used to bind the procuring entity party; its meaning is already carried by CONTRACT_PROCURED_BY, so a separate role claim would duplicate it.
supplier_party.idAn opaque publisher party key with no business meaning. ABN is the published identifier where available, and a source-specific party key concept would duplicate identity without adding evidence; it must never be treated as a legal identifier.
procuring_entity_party.idAn opaque publisher party key with no business meaning; it must never be treated as a legal identifier.
supplier_party.contactPoint.emailHeld-out rows show the supplier party's contactPoint is the buyer's boilerplate (e.g. a Marshall Islands supplier carrying procurement@niaa.gov.au), so mapping it to the supplier entity would assert a wrong-entity email. Unmapped rather than attached to the wrong business.
supplier_party.contactPoint.telephoneHeld-out rows show the supplier party's contactPoint telephone is the buyer's boilerplate (e.g. '02 6261 1111' on a foreign supplier), so mapping it to the supplier entity would assert a wrong-entity phone. Unmapped rather than attached to the wrong business.
procuring_entity_party.contactPoint.emailThe procuring entity publishes a role mailbox (tenders@finance.gov.au), but the role_email transform classifies it as a personal mailbox and drops it (18/18 dropped in validation). Rather than claim a concept that silently discards the value, the field is unmapped and the transform misclassification is recorded as a finding.
ocidocid identifies an OCDS contracting process or package that can span multiple releases, awards and contracts, not a single contract or supplier. At this one-release-per-row grain the release id already keys the record and there is no contracting-process entity type.
tender.idtender.id repeats the ocid in this snapshot, so it is a process-level key rather than a tender or contract identifier.
tender.procurementMethodDetailsA human-readable restatement of procurementMethod ('Limited tender', 'Open tender'); storing both would create a redundant alias of dyn.contract.procurement_method.
contracts[0].titleIn this snapshot title holds short internal reference strings (e.g. NCD11811, 4600030790) that look like agency references rather than descriptive titles; mapping it would create a second identifier concept indistinguishable from dyn.contract.notice_number.
contracts[0].awardIDA local join key from contract to award inside the release, not a business fact; the supplier link it supports is already recorded by CONTRACT_AWARDED_TO.
awards[0].idA local award identifier scoped to the release and publisher, not a business identifier and not stable across sources.
awards[0].statusAward status uses a separate OCDS codelist and in this snapshot merely repeats contracts[0].status; mapping both would create two competing status claims for one notice until they are reconciled.
awards[0].dateIn the observed non-amendment rows the award date equals the contract dateSigned, so it adds no separate business fact there; on amendment releases it does not equal the release date, so it is not a publication timestamp either. A third competing date for one notice would be misleading. This is a bounded coverage choice based on observed rows, not a claim that the two dates are always equal.
awards[0].suppliers[0].idA denormalised restatement of the supplier party id; the supplier link is already carried by CONTRACT_AWARDED_TO and the party binding, so a separate claim would duplicate identity.
awards[0].suppliers[0].nameA denormalised restatement of parties[].name for the same supplier; mapping both would double-count one name claim.
contracts[0].items[0].idAn internal item identifier local to the release; it is not a business identifier and adds nothing beyond the item classification code already captured.
supplier_party.contactPoint.namecontactPoint.name mixes organisational unit labels ('FINANCE DIVISION', 'Procurement', 'CONTRACTS OFFICER') with personal names ('Sandra Tuck'). The ontology has no concept for a contact's unit, and personal names must not be stored, so these are dropped rather than mapped.
supplier_party.contactPoint.branchA contact organisational branch label for the party (e.g. 'Budget and Policy Branch'), not a business fact and not a concept in the current ontology.
supplier_party.contactPoint.divisionA contact organisational division label for the party (e.g. 'FINANCE DIVISION'), not a business fact and not a concept in the current ontology.
supplier_party.address.streetAddressA single street-address line (e.g. 'PO Box 3'), often a post-office box. address.full expects a complete formatted address and concatenating lines would invent structure the source does not state.
procuring_entity_party.address.streetAddressA single street-address line for a government agency; the same objection as for supplier street addresses applies, and address.full is not a street-line concept.
procuring_entity_party.address.countryNameNot published for the procuring entity party in this snapshot; the column profile shows no nonempty values, so no country claim can be made without inventing data.
procuring_entity_party.contactPoint.telephoneNot published for the procuring entity party in this snapshot; no phone claim can be made without inventing data.
tagConstant ('contract') schema metadata describing the OCDS release stage, not a business fact; the contract entity already carries that meaning.
initiationTypeConstant ('tender') OCDS schema metadata describing how the process was initiated, with no business fact about the supplier or the contract.
languageA document language tag ('EN') for the release payload, not a claim about any business or contract.

      "Ownership error on contact data: mapping fields `supplier_party.contactPoint.email` -> `ext.contact.role_email` and `supplier_party.contactPoint.telephone` -> `ext.contact.phone` bind the supplier party's contact point, but held-out rows show the supplier contactPoint is the buyer's boilerplate (ordinal 15: supplier 'RMI Cleaning Services, LLC' carries contactPoint.telephone '02 6261 1111', branch 'Budget and Policy Branch', division 'FINANCE DIVISION'; ordinal 19/24/26/27 identical). Emitted example `b036d77ce7e7e562ce9870e2` attaches +61262611111 to entity_ref business:austender_new:EXEMPTUUID-10663AC5723C37E191F3E7D32B7994B2 (the Marshall Islands supplier). This is a wrong-entity claim and must be fixed or the fields unmapped.",
      "Procuring-entity contact email is silently dropped: validation shows `procuring_entity_party.contactPoint.email` source_present 18, emitted 0, dropped PERSONAL_MAILBOX_DROPPED 18, yet held-out rows show 'tenders@finance.gov.au' (a role mailbox) at parties[1].contactPoint.email. The role_email transform is misclassifying a role mailbox as personal, so source-stated new information is not emitted. Either fix the transform or record the drop as a finding; do not claim the concept 'already stores role mailboxes' when it drops them.",
      "`dyn.contract.limited_tender_reason_code` source_paths include `tender.limitedTenderReason` but the mapping field only reads `tender.limitedTenderReasonCode`; the concept's source_paths list is inconsistent with the mapping and the definition's claim that 'the verbatim reason text quotes' the code is an unsupported semantic assumption (no evidence the reason text contains the code).",
      "`dyn.contract.value_currency` duplicates the currency qualifier on `dyn.contract.value` (both read contracts[0].value.currency). This is a redundant concept: the qualifier already carries the currency, and a separate claim creates two competing currency facts for one contract. Either drop the concept or justify why a qualifier cannot be read without a standalone claim.",
      "`dyn.contract.item_classification_code` is declared multi:true but the mapping reads only `contracts[0].items[0].classification.id` (first item). If a contract has multiple items, only the first is captured and the multi flag is misleading; the mapping should iterate items or the concept should be single with an explicit first-item caveat.",
      "`dyn.contract.notice_number` is keyed from `contracts[0].id` and used as the contract entity key, but held-out ordinal 19 shows a contractAmendment release (tag 'contractAmendment', amendments[0].id 'CN4280171-A1') with the same contracts[0].id 'CN4280171' as the original. The notice_number therefore does not uniquely identify a release/amendment; the entity key collides across the original and its amendment, and the definition's claim that it 'links amendments' is not supported by a distinct amendment identifier.",

  "critic": {
    "verdict": "revise",
    "findings": [
      "Ownership/entity.legal_name: mapping emits supplier_party.name and procuring_entity_party.name into entity.legal_name, whose canonical definition is 'As registered. Not the trading name.' The proposal itself concedes the publisher does not distinguish registered from trading names (mapping.fields[0].rationale, reuse.entity.legal_name). Emitting an unverified party name under a concept defined as the registered name is a semantic mismatch; the emitted observation carries canonical_definition 'As registered. Not the trading name.' with value 'RMI Cleaning Services, LLC' and 'Lander and Rogers'. Either use entity.trading_name or a party-name concept, or record an explicit claim_scope that bounds it as a published party name.",
      "Precision loss vs concept type: dyn.contract.signed_at is declared type 'datetime' and defined as 'kept at the source's stated precision', but the mapping uses parse_date and the emitted value is '2026-10-01' (emitted_examples field dyn.contract.signed_at, raw_value '2026-10-01T07:06:03Z'). The concept definition and the emitted type disagree; the definition must be corrected to date or the transform must preserve time.",
      "Timezone assumption: dyn.contract.start_date/end_date definitions assert a 'local-midnight artefact for an Australian (AEST/UTC+10)' reading of 14:00Z. Held-out ordinal 15 is a Marshall Islands supplier with period 2026-08-31T14:00:00Z; the assumption is stated as bounded but is not evidenced by the publisher. It is acceptable only if explicitly labelled an unverified convention, not a fact about the contract.",
      "Contract entity key collision: entities.contract.key_paths is contracts[0].id, and held-out ordinal 19 is tag 'contractAmendment' with contracts[0].id 'CN4280171' and amendments[0].amendsReleaseID pointing to an earlier release. The original and amendment releases therefore produce the same contract entity_ref 'contract:austender_new:CN4280171' with potentially different values (e.g. value, period). The proposal documents the collision but does not resolve it; downstream this silently merges or overwrites two distinct publication states.",
      "Amendment semantics not emitted: held-out ordinal 19 carries contracts[0].amendments[0].id, releaseID and amendsReleaseID, and tag 'contractAmendment'. The mapping emits neither the amendment identity nor the amended-release link, so source-stated new information about which release amends which is dropped. This is a coverage gap, not merely an unmapped field, because it is the only way to disambiguate the colliding contract key.",
      "Role binding vs award supplier: CONTRACT_AWARDED_TO requires awards[0].suppliers[0].id to equal the role-bound supplier party id. Held-out rows show a single supplier, but the relationship definition itself allows multiple suppliers and the mapping reads only awards[0].suppliers[0] and a single supplier_party binding. If a release has two suppliers, the binding must refuse and the relationship must not be emitted; the mapping does not state that the relationship is suppressed when the supplier binding refuses.",
      "Procuring entity address coverage: validation shows procuring_entity address.locality source_present 0/18, address.state 2/18, address.postcode 2/18. The mapping still declares these fields for procuring_entity; that is harmless but the proposal should state that procuring-entity address claims are near-empty and must not be read as 'no address'.",
      "Supplier contactPoint: held-out ordinals 15, 19, 24, 26, 27 all show supplier contactPoint.telephone '02 6261 1111', branch 'Budget and Policy Branch', division 'FINANCE DIVISION' and email '' — i.e. the buyer's boilerplate on the supplier party. The proposal correctly unmaps supplier email/telephone, but the reuse section still lists ext.contact.role_email and ext.contact.phone as reused for parties[].contactPoint generally; that reuse statement is now contradicted by the mapping and should be removed or scoped to procuring entities only.",
      "Procuring entity role mailbox dropped: held-out rows show procuring_entity contactPoint.email 'tenders@finance.gov.au', a role mailbox, yet the proposal states the role_email transform classifies it as personal and drops it (18/18). The field is unmapped with that reason, which is honest, but a source-stated role mailbox is being discarded by a transform misclassification; this is a fixable loss, not an inherent unmappable.",
      "Supplier ABN coverage: validation shows supplier entity.abn source_present 16/18, emitted 16/18. Held-out ordinals 15 and 24 are EXEMPTUUID suppliers with no additionalIdentifiers, so absence is genuine. The mapping reads only additionalIdentifiers[0]; a party whose ABN is at index 1 would be silently missed. This is documented but remains a bounded risk.",
      "address.state coercion: held-out ordinal 15 region 'Outside Australia' is emitted as address.state 'OTHER' (emitted_examples address.state, raw_value 'Outside Australia'). The enum has OTHER, so this is acceptable, but the raw region is lost and the proposal should note that 'OTHER' conflates all non-Australian regions.",
      "Foreign postcode: held-out ordinals 15 ('96960') and 24 ('22102-1036') are emitted as address.postcode unchanged. The proposal correctly avoids the AU postcode transform; this is consistent and bounded.",
      "Contract value: held-out values are AUD maximums (e.g. 13221.72, 55000.00, 132327.36) with currency qualifier AUD emitted. The proposal correctly does not treat these as business income. No error found here.",
      "UNSPSC item code: held-out codes 76110000, 80120000, 80111600, 81110000 are emitted with scheme UNSPSC and not mapped to entity.industry_code. Correct; no ANZSIC crosswalk is invented.",
      "Procuring entity country/telephone: the proposal unmaps procuring_entity_party.address.countryName and contactPoint.telephone as 'not present in the inspected bindings'. Held-out rows show procuring_entity has no countryName and no telephone, so the absence is genuine, but the reason wording ('not present in the inspected bindings') is a data-availability statement, not a semantic reason; it should be phrased as 'not published for this party in the snapshot'.",
      "Observation.observed_at: mapping uses releases[].date with parse_date, and emitted observed_at is '2026-10-01T00:00:00+00:00' for a raw date '2026-10-01T07:06:03Z'. The time of day is truncated; observed_at is a datetime concept, so this is a precision loss that should be stated.",
      "Contract status enum: only 'active' is observed; the proposal states the other enum values are unverified. Held-out rows all show 'active', so no contradiction, but the enum values pending/cancelled/terminated are asserted without source evidence and should be marked as codelist-derived, not observed.",
      "limited_tender_exempt: held-out rows show '1' only; the '0' mapping is unverified. The proposal states this. No error, but the enum 'false' value is not evidenced in the snapshot.",
      "limited_tender_reason_code/reason: only 1/18 rows carry these; held-out examples do not show them. The proposal states the mapping is unverified on that row. Acceptable as bounded, but the concept should not be presented as validated."
    ],
    "semantic_risks": [
      "entity.legal_name is defined as the registered name but is populated with a publisher party name that may be a trading name or a trust name; downstream consumers may treat it as a legal name.",
      "dyn.contract.signed_at is typed datetime but emitted as a date, so the concept definition and the data disagree.",
      "The contract entity key contracts[0].id collides across original and amendment releases; without an amendment identity or release-scoped key, two publication states of one notice are merged.",
      "The AEST/UTC+10 local-midnight reading of 14:00Z is an unverified convention applied to non-Australian suppliers (e.g. Marshall Islands), risking an off-by-one day.",
      "The CONTRACT_AWARDED_TO relationship is defined to require an id match but the mapping does not state that the relationship is suppressed when the supplier binding refuses or when multiple suppliers exist.",
      "The reuse section still claims ext.contact.role_email and ext.contact.phone for parties[].contactPoint, contradicting the mapping's unmapping of supplier contactPoint due to buyer boilerplate.",
      "The procuring entity role mailbox tenders@finance.gov.au is dropped by a transform misclassification, losing a source-stated role contact.",
      "address.state 'OTHER' conflates all non-Australian regions and loses the raw region text.",
      "observation.observed_at truncates the publication time of day."
    ],
    "checked_reuse": [
      "entity.abn: reused for AU-ABN additionalIdentifiers with checksum transform; correct, and held-out ABNs 47065634525 and 58207240529 are emitted. No legal form inferred.",
      "entity.legal_name: reuse is questionable because the canonical definition is 'As registered. Not the trading name.' and the publisher does not distinguish registered from trading names.",
      "address.locality/state/postcode/country: reused for the party's own stated address; correct ownership, with the state OTHER coercion and foreign postcode retention noted.",
      "observation.source_record_id: reused for releases[].id; correct, since the row grain is one release.",
      "observation.observed_at: reused for releases[].date; correct concept, but time-of-day truncation noted.",
      "observation.source_id and observation.licence: reused from dataset metadata; correct.",
      "ext.contact.role_email and ext.contact.phone: listed as reused in the proposal but contradicted by the mapping's unmapping of supplier contactPoint; reuse should be removed or scoped to procuring entities.",
      "ext.financial.*: correctly not reused for contract value; contract value is a different entity and a maximum over life, not income.",
      "ext.entity.industry_code and entity.industry_code: correctly not reused for UNSPSC item codes; no crosswalk invented.",
      "entity.status: correctly not reused for contract status; contract status is a different entity.",
      "ext.licence.start_date/end_date: correctly not reused for contract period dates.",
      "entity.date_registered and ext.entity.date_established: correctly not reused for contract signed_at or period dates.",
      "observation.valid_from/valid_to: correctly not reused for contract period; they describe claim validity, not contract term."
    ],
    "rationale": "The proposal is largely careful about ownership, units, and entity separation: contract value is not business income, UNSPSC is not ANZSIC, contract status is not entity status, and contract dates are not registration or licence dates. However, it must be revised before acceptance because (1) entity.legal_name is defined as the registered name but is populated with a publisher party name the proposal itself says may be a trading or trust name; (2) dyn.contract.signed_at is typed datetime but emitted as a date; (3) the contract entity key contracts[0].id collides across original and amendment releases (held-out ordinal 19), and the amendment identity/amendsReleaseID is not emitted, so source-stated new information is dropped and two publication states are merged; (4) the reuse section still claims ext.contact.role_email and ext.contact.phone for parties[].contactPoint, contradicting the mapping's unmapping of supplier contactPoint due to buyer boilerplate; (5) the AEST/UTC+10 local-midnight convention is unverified and applied to non-Australian suppliers. Remaining risks (supplier ABN at index >0, state OTHER conflation, foreign postcodes, unverified enum values, procuring-entity address sparsity, observed_at time truncation) are bounded and documented. This verdict is an agent review, not human sign-off."
  }
}

{
  "verdict": "revise",
  "findings": [
    "Checked the withdrawn amendment concepts: contracts[0].amendments[0].id, .releaseID and .amendsReleaseID are now unmapped, and held-out row ordinal 19 (tag 'contractAmendment') shows those paths do carry values. The unmapped reasons for all three say 'not present in the inspected snapshot (no nonempty values in the column profile)', which is contradicted by the held-out evidence. This is a false statement about the source, not a coverage gap.",
    "The same held-out row (ordinal 19) is an amendment release whose contracts[0].id is 'CN4280171' and whose contracts[0].amendments[0].releaseID equals the release id. dyn.contract.notice_number is defined as 'not unique per release because an amendment release restates the original notice id', and the contract entity key_paths are ['contracts[0].id']. The engine's named-role identity may repeat across records, so this is a bounded recall/identity limitation rather than an overwrite, but the proposal should state that the contract role key collides across the original and amendment releases and that the amendment release is not distinguishable by the contract key alone.",
    "dyn.contract.start_date / dyn.contract.end_date: the definition says the source states 14:00Z and that reading it as local midnight is an unverified convention. Held-out evidence shows endDate '2026-12-30T13:00:00Z' (ordinal 27), i.e. the time component is not constant. The stated '14:00Z' premise is wrong, and the parse_date transform truncates the time, so the emitted date is the UTC calendar date, not a local-midnight reading. The definition must be corrected to say the emitted value is the UTC date component of the stated timestamp and that the local contract day may differ.",
    "dyn.contract.signed_at: held-out ordinal 19 shows contracts[0].dateSigned '2026-10-01T07:06:02Z' while awards[0].date is '2026-10-01T07:06:02Z' and the release date is '2026-10-21T05:28:07Z'. The unmapped reason for awards[0].date says 'In this snapshot the award date equals the contract dateSigned', which is true for that row, but the claim that it 'adds no separate business fact' is asserted from a single equality and is not a semantic error; it is a bounded coverage choice. No blocking change required, but the reason should be scoped to observed rows.",
    "dyn.contract.limited_tender_exempt: the definition says only '1' is observed and 'false' is codelist-derived, but field_evidence shows a 'false' emission from raw '0' at source_row_ordinal 31. The definition is contradicted by the evidence; 'false' is observed.",
    "dyn.contract.limited_tender_reason_code / dyn.contract.limited_tender_reason: definitions say 'Only one row in this snapshot carries it, so the mapping is unverified on that row.' Validation shows source_present 1 and emitted 1 with error_rate 0.0, and field_evidence shows the emitted value 'TR' and the verbatim reason text. The mapping is verified on the one observed row; the 'unverified' wording overstates uncertainty and should be replaced with 'observed on one row only'.",
    "address.state@supplier: held-out ordinal 15 shows raw 'Outside Australia' emitted as 'OTHER'. The reuse note says non-Australian regions are 'coerced to OTHER, which conflates all non-Australian regions and loses the raw text'. That is an accurate bounded statement of a lossy transform, not an incorrect claim, but the emitted 'OTHER' must not be read as an Australian state; the concept definition for address.state is empty in the ontology, so the mapping should state that OTHER means 'not an Australian state/territory' rather than a location.",
    "entity.legal_name@supplier: held-out ordinal 15 emits 'RMI Cleaning Services, LLC' and ordinal 24 emits 'MILES AND STOCKBRIDGE P.C.' as entity.legal_name with claim_scope null. The reuse note says the publisher does not distinguish registered from trading names and no current_legal_name claim_scope is set, but entity.legal_name is defined as 'As registered. Not the trading name.' Emitting a publisher party name into a concept defined as the registered legal name, with no claim_scope, is a semantic mismatch: the value is a published party name, not a registrar-stated legal name. This is the strongest remaining issue.",
    "entity.abn@supplier: held-out ordinal 15 has no additionalIdentifiers and no ABN emitted (source_present 16/18, emitted 16). That is an expected abstention, not an error. The abn transform is applied and validation shows 0 value errors, so the checksum guard is exercised; no blocking finding.",
    "CONTRACT_AWARDED_TO: the relationship definition says the link is established by matching awards[0].suppliers[0].id to the role-bound supplier party id and that divergence must be refused. The mapping's own rationale admits 'no extra equality guard is executed, so a divergence between the award supplier id and the party id is not detected by the config.' The definition therefore describes a guard the config does not implement. Held-out rows 15, 19 and 24 show the ids equal, so no wrong edge is evidenced, but the definition must be rewritten to match the executed behaviour (edge emits when both roles emitted claims) rather than claiming an equality guard.",
    "dyn.contract.value: held-out values 13221.72, 55000, 44072.4 with currency AUD qualifier. The definition correctly says total maximum over contract life, not annual expenditure or business income. No blocking finding.",
    "dyn.contract.item_classification_code: held-out values 76110000, 80120000, 80111600 with scheme UNSPSC. The definition correctly bounds this to the contracted item, not the supplier's industry. No blocking finding.",
    "dyn.contract.procurement_method: held-out 'limited' and 'open' map correctly; the definition correctly bounds it to the procurement process. No blocking finding.",
    "procuring_entity address fields: validation shows locality source_present 0, state/postcode source_present 2, countryName and telephone unmapped as not published. These are expected abstentions, not incorrect claims.",
    "procuring_entity_party.contactPoint.email is unmapped with the reason that role_email drops tenders@finance.gov.au 18/18. Held-out rows confirm the value is a role mailbox.     "supplier contactPoint email/telephone unmapped: held-out ordinal 15 shows a Marshall Islands supplier carrying '02 6261 1111' and empty email; ordinal 19 shows a Canberra supplier with the same buyer boilerplate phone. The wrong-entity rationale is supported by evidence. No blocking finding.",
    "observation.observed_at uses releases[].date with parse_date truncation; held-out ordinal 19 release date '2026-10-21T05:28:07Z' differs from contract dateSigned '2026-10-01T07:06:02Z', so publication and signing are correctly kept distinct. No blocking finding.",
    "record_id strategy 'columns' on ['id'] with the note that the engine uses a content hash for source_record_id regardless. This matches the execution contract; no hidden index assumption.",
    "No invented currency, scheme or legal form is emitted: currency comes from contracts[0].value.currency, scheme from classification.scheme, and no entity_type or legal_form claim is made from party names such as 'The Trustee for ... Trust' or 'Data#3 Limited'."
  ],
  "semantic_risks": [
    "Amendment releases (tag 'contractAmendment') are not distinguishable from original releases by the contract role key contracts[0].id; downstream consumers joining on dyn.contract.notice_number will see the original and amendment as the same contract notice. This is a bounded identity limitation, not an overwrite, but it should be documented.",
    "dyn.contract.start_date / end_date emit the UTC date component; for a contract whose local period boundary is not UTC midnight the emitted day may be off by one. The corrected definition should keep this bounded.",
    "address.state 'OTHER' conflates all non-Australian regions and loses the raw region text; consumers must not read OTHER as an Australian state.",
    "entity.legal_name is populated with publisher party names that are not registrar-stated legal names; if a downstream consumer treats entity.legal_name as authoritative registered names, it will over-claim.",
    "Contract value is a reported maximum over the contract life; any downstream aggregation as annual expenditure or supplier revenue would be wrong, though the definition already warns against it.",
    "UNSPSC item classification is a contract-item code, not the supplier's company-wide industry; no crosswalk exists and none is claimed.",
    "ABN presence is not universal (16/18 suppliers); absence is an abstention, not evidence the supplier has no ABN."
  ],
  "checked_reuse": [
    "entity.legal_name: checked; reuse is semantically mismatched because the concept is defined as the registered legal name and the source states a publisher party name. Flagged as a blocking finding.",
    "entity.abn: checked; checksum-validated, scheme-gated on AU-ABN, no legal-form inference. Correct reuse.",
    "address.locality / address.state / address.postcode / address.country: checked; owned by business and describe the party's own stated address. Correct reuse, with the OTHER conflation bounded.",
    "observation.source_record_id / observed_at / source_id / licence: checked; release id, release date, dataset id and cc-by licence id are the right concepts. Correct reuse.",
    "ext.contact.role_email / ext.contact.phone: checked; only proposed for the procuring entity role and then unmapped because the transform drops the role mailbox. No wrong-entity reuse.",
    "ext.financial.total_income / taxable_income / tax_payable / revenue_band: checked and correctly not reused for contract value.",
    "ext.entity.industry_code / entity.industry_code: checked and correctly not reused for UNSPSC item classification.",
    "entity.status: checked and correctly not reused for contract status.",
    "ext.licence.start_date / end_date: checked and correctly not reused for contract period dates.",
    "ext.entity.sector_tag: checked and correctly not reused for contract description.",
    "dyn.contract.* concepts: checked for duplicate concepts; notice_number, signed_at, start_date, end_date, description, value, status, item_classification_code, procurement_method, limited_tender_exempt, limited_tender_reason_code and limited_tender_reason are distinct and do not duplicate each other or existing concepts."
  ],
  "rationale": "The mapping is largely honest and well-bounded: contract value is not folded into business income, UNSPSC is not promoted to supplier industry, ABNs are checksum-gated, supplier contact points are unmapped as buyer boilerplate, and amendment fields are no longer proposed as concepts. However, several definitions and unmapped reasons are contradicted by the held-out evidence and validation: (1) the amendment unmapped reasons claim the fields are absent from the snapshot, but held-out ordinal 19 carries them; (2) limited_tender_exempt says only '1' is observed, but a '0' emission is evidenced; (3) the limited-tender reason fields are called 'unverified' although they were emitted and validated; (4) the contract period definitions assert a constant 14:00Z local-midnight convention that the 13:00Z endDate contradicts; (5) CONTRACT_AWARDED_TO's definition claims an equality guard the config does not execute; and (6) entity.legal_name is populated with publisher party names despite being defined as the registered legal name. These are correctable definition/scope errors rather than wrong-owner or invented-fact errors, so the verdict is revise with the listed blocking findings.",

      "Checked the withdrawn amendment concepts: contracts[0].amendments[0].id, .releaseID and .amendsReleaseID are now unmapped, and held-out row ordinal 19 (tag 'contractAmendment') shows those paths do carry values. The unmapped reasons for all three say 'not present in the inspected snapshot (no nonempty values in the column profile)', which is contradicted by the held-out evidence. This is a false statement about the source, not a coverage gap.",
      "The same held-out row (ordinal 19) is an amendment release whose contracts[0].id is 'CN4280171' and whose contracts[0].amendments[0].releaseID equals the release id. dyn.contract.notice_number is defined as 'not unique per release because an amendment release restates the original notice id', and the contract entity key_paths are ['contracts[0].id']. The engine's named-role identity may repeat across records, so this is a bounded recall/identity limitation rather than an overwrite, but the proposal should state that the contract role key collides across the original and amendment releases and that the amendment release is not distinguishable by the contract key alone.",
      "dyn.contract.start_date / dyn.contract.end_date: the definition says the source states 14:00Z and that reading it as local midnight is an unverified convention. Held-out evidence shows endDate '2026-12-30T13:00:00Z' (ordinal 27), i.e. the time component is not constant. The stated '14:00Z' premise is wrong, and the parse_date transform truncates the time, so the emitted date is the UTC calendar date, not a local-midnight reading. The definition must be corrected to say the emitted value is the UTC date component of the stated timestamp and that the local contract day may differ.",
      "dyn.contract.signed_at: held-out ordinal 19 shows contracts[0].dateSigned '2026-10-01T07:06:02Z' while awards[0].date is '2026-10-01T07:06:02Z' and the release date is '2026-10-21T05:28:07Z'. The unmapped reason for awards[0].date says 'In this snapshot the award date equals the contract dateSigned', which is true for that row, but the claim that it 'adds no separate business fact' is asserted from a single equality and is not a semantic error; it is a bounded coverage choice. No blocking change required, but the reason should be scoped to observed rows.",
      "dyn.contract.limited_tender_exempt: the definition says only '1' is observed and 'false' is codelist-derived, but field_evidence shows a 'false' emission from raw '0' at source_row_ordinal 31. The definition is contradicted by the evidence; 'false' is observed.",
      "dyn.contract.limited_tender_reason_code / dyn.contract.limited_tender_reason: definitions say 'Only one row in this snapshot carries it, so the mapping is unverified on that row.' The mapping is verified on the one observed row; the 'unverified' wording overstates uncertainty and should be replaced with 'observed on one row only'.",
      "address.state@supplier: held-out ordinal 15 shows raw 'Outside Australia' emitted as 'OTHER'. The reuse note says non-Australian regions are 'coerced to OTHER, which conflates all non-Australian regions and loses the raw text'. That is an accurate bounded statement of a lossy transform, not an incorrect claim, but the emitted 'OTHER' must not be read as an Australian state; the concept definition for address.state is empty in the ontology, so the mapping should state that OTHER means 'not an Australian state/territory' rather than a location.",
      "entity.legal_name@supplier: held-out ordinal 15 emits 'RMI Cleaning Services, LLC' and ordinal 24 emits 'MILES AND STOCKBRIDGE P.C.' as entity.legal_name with claim_scope null. The reuse note says the publisher does not distinguish registered from trading names and no current_legal_name claim_scope is set, but entity.legal_name is defined as 'As registered. Not the trading name.' Emitting a publisher party name into a concept defined as the registered legal name, with no claim_scope, is a semantic mismatch: the value is a published party name, not a registrar-stated legal name. This is the strongest remaining issue.",
      "entity.abn@supplier: held-out ordinal 15 has no additionalIdentifiers and no ABN emitted (source_present 16/18, emitted 16). That is an expected abstention, not an error.       "CONTRACT_AWARDED_TO: the relationship definition says the link is established by matching awards[0].suppliers[0].id to the role-bound supplier party id and that divergence must be refused. The definition therefore describes a guard the config does not implement. Held-out rows 15, 19 and 24 show the ids equal, so no wrong edge is evidenced, but the definition must be rewritten to match the executed behaviour (edge emits when both roles emitted claims) rather than claiming an equality guard.",
      "dyn.contract.value: held-out values 13221.72, 55000, 44072.4 with currency AUD qualifier. The definition correctly says total maximum over contract life, not annual expenditure or business income. No blocking finding.",

      "dyn.contract.notice_number as contract role key collides across original and amendment releases (held-out ordinal 19). The engine's named-role identity may repeat across records; downstream consumers must not treat the contract key as unique per release. Disclosed, but a real identity limitation.",
      "entity.legal_name reuse for supplier and procuring-entity party names is a published-name approximation, not registrar-stated legal name; no claim_scope is set. Downstream must not treat it as authoritative registered-name evidence.",
      "address.state OTHER conflates all non-Australian regions and loses the raw text; it must not be read as an Australian state. Disclosed.",
      "CONTRACT_AWARDED_TO has no executed equality guard between awards[0].suppliers[0].id and the role-bound supplier party id; a divergence would not be detected by the config.       "Only the first item of contracts[0].items is read; additional items are not captured. This is a bounded coverage limitation, not an incorrect emitted claim.",
      "dyn.contract.limited_tender_reason_code and limited_tender_reason are observed on a single row (ordinal 31); coverage is limited and the mapping is verified only on that observation.",
      "procuring_entity_party.address.locality is 0/18 present and countryName/telephone are not published; these are expected abstentions, not missing coverage errors.",
      "The role_email transform misclassifies the procuring entity's role mailbox (tenders@finance.gov.au) as personal and drops it; the field is unmapped rather than claimed, so no wrong claim is emitted, but the transform behaviour is a recorded finding for the engine owner."
    ],
    "checked_reuse": [
      "entity.legal_name: checked for legal-form inference and registered-vs-trading-name conflation; proposal scopes it as published party name with no claim_scope and no legal-form inference.",
      "entity.abn: checked for checksum validation and scheme gating; abn transform applied, scheme == AU-ABN gate present, absence treated as abstention.",
      "address.locality/state/postcode/country: checked for wrong-entity attachment and foreign-value coercion; attached to the party's own stated address, OTHER and foreign postcodes disclosed.",
      "observation.source_record_id/observed_at/source_id/licence: checked for grain and time-scope correctness; release id is the row grain, release date is publication not valid_from, licence id 'cc-by' from metadata.",
      "ext.contact.role_email/phone: checked for wrong-entity reuse; not reused for supplier parties (buyer boilerplate), and procuring-entity email left unmapped due to transform drop.",
      "entity.status, ext.entity.legal_form, ext.entity.status_detail, ext.entity.date_established, ext.licence.start_date/end_date, ext.financial.*, ext.entity.industry_code, entity.industry_code, ext.entity.sector_tag, ext.licence.authorisation_text: checked and correctly not reused for contract status, contract dates, contract value, item classification, contract description or procurement reason.",
      "dyn.contract.* concepts: checked for duplicate concepts and wrong owner; each is contract-owned and distinct from business-owned concepts."
    ],
    "rationale": "The proposal and mapping are internally consistent with the publisher metadata, validation results and held-out rows. Contract facts are contract-owned; business facts are business-owned; no contract value is folded into business income; no UNSPSC item code is promoted to supplier industry; no legal form is inferred from party names or ABNs; ABNs are checksum-validated and scheme-gated. The amendment unmapped reasons now correctly acknowledge the held-out amendment observation and explain the bounded reason for leaving those fields unmapped. No evidenced semantic error, wrong owner, invented scheme/currency, contradictory definition or unimplemented guard remains. Grants — Recipients

Funding awards, programmes, project descriptions, recipient names and ABNs. Includes individuals and community organisations; do not infer all recipients are companies.

Downloaded source · Publisher documentation · CC-BY-4.0

Publisher: Brisbane City Council. Retrieved 2026-11-22T22:37:19+00:00. Snapshot e09508378cb0071d293488d0fcf12c45d0099dcaca7b351dde2b717fa496f643

New ontology concepts

ConceptOwnerTypeMeaning
dyn.grant_award.amount_approvedgrant_awarddecimalThe funding amount approved for a single grant award, as published by the funding body. This is an award amount, not business income, revenue or turnover. The source does not state a currency; the currency qualifier is an inference from the Brisbane City Council publisher jurisdiction and is recorded as currency_basis=inferred, never as publisher-stated.
dyn.grant_award.roundgrant_awardstringThe funding round or financial-year label under which the award was made, in the publisher's wording (e.g. 2025-26). Identifies the award cycle, not a reporting period of the recipient.
dyn.grant_award.project_descriptiongrant_awardstringThe publisher's free-text description of the project or activity the award funds. Source-stated activity text; do not convert into an industry code.
dyn.grant_award.recipient_name_as_publishedgrant_awardstringThe recipient name exactly as the funding body published it. The population is mixed: natural persons (e.g. 'Ms Alexandra Philp'), incorporated community organisations (e.g. 'Stones Corner Community Kindergarten Incorporated', 'The Rotary Club of Nundah Inc') and council administrative units such as ward offices (e.g. 'Moorooka Ward Office', 'McDowall Ward Office'). Matching evidence only; never implies legal form, company status or organisation kind. The source does not state recipient kind, so no kind is inferred from the name. Personal-data classification is unresolved: the value is personal when the recipient is a natural person and not personal when it is an organisation or council unit, and the source does not let us distinguish, so it is treated conservatively as potentially personal rather than asserted to be personal.
dyn.grant_award.recipient_abngrant_awardstringThe Australian Business Number the publisher recorded against the award recipient, checksum-validated. An ABN is not a company number and its presence does not establish company status or legal form. Personal-data classification is unresolved: an ABN can belong to an individual (sole trader) as well as an organisation, and recipient kind is not source-stated, so it is not asserted to be non-personal. No checksum-invalid value was observed in the validated snapshot (85 present, 85 emitted, 0 value errors), so no separate invalid-ABN report is produced in this pass; a future invalid value would be dropped by the abn transform and would not be counted separately.
dyn.grant_programme.namegrant_programmestringThe published name of the grant programme or scheme under which awards are made, in the funding body's wording, kept verbatim. No suffix is stripped and no ward/locality meaning is asserted, because the source does not state that a ' - <suffix>' component is a delivery location rather than a distinct sub-programme. The label is not a canonical programme key: typographic variants (ASCII vs curly apostrophe, hyphen vs en dash) are not normalised, so the same programme can appear under more than one label and therefore as more than one programme entity.
dyn.grant_programme.codegrant_programmestringThe short code the publisher prefixes to the programme label in the combined grant_list field (e.g. L, C), extracted by regex from the text before the first comma. Programme classification code, not a company-wide attribute. The extraction is unguarded: no transform in the available allowlist can check that the label after the comma equals the grant field, so a mismatched grant_list/grant pair would silently yield a wrong code. The value is therefore unverified publisher-prefix evidence, not a verified programme key, and is emitted at reduced confidence.
dyn.grant_programme.groupgrant_programmestringThe broader programme group or category the publisher assigns to the grant programme (e.g. Creative and history grants, Community grants), in the publisher's wording.

Reuse and explicit omissions

Existing conceptJustification
entity.abnThe award's abn value can populate entity.abn once the recipient is resolved to a business entity and the ABN checksum validates; the raw award-level value is retained in dyn.grant_award.recipient_abn.
ext.entity.holder_kindThe published recipient string can be classified as individual/organisation/unknown as matching evidence, without implying sole_trader or legal form. Not emitted in this pass.
observation.source_record_idThe export 'index' field is not a durable key; a deterministic record id is constructed from stable field values (programme, round, recipient, project, amount) with no normalisation applied, matching the field transforms. Typographic variants (ASCII vs curly apostrophe, hyphen vs en dash) therefore yield distinct ids; this is a documented limitation, not a claimed stability guarantee.
observation.licenceDataset metadata states CC-BY-4.0; record it on observations.
observation.observed_atNo source-stated observation date exists in the dataset. observed_at is left null rather than populated with the retrieval time; the retrieval time is recorded only in observation.ingested_at, so a source-stated observation time is never fabricated.
Columns deliberately left unmapped
ColumnReason
indexExport row ordinal that may change between snapshots; not a stable business key, so it is not modelled. A deterministic record id is constructed from stable award attributes instead.
Field coverage and rejected values
TargetRoleSource presentEmittedErrorsDeliberate drops

0-critic.json

{
  "verdict": "revise",
  "findings": [
    "Record identity is not stable across the snapshot: `mapping.record_id.source_paths` = [\"grant\", \"grant_round\", \"organisation_recipient\", \"project\", \"amount_approved\"] and `entities.grant_award.key_paths` mirror this, but `grant` embeds ward/locality text that varies in punctuation and dash style (e.g. \"Lord Mayor's Community Fund - Coorparoo\" vs \"Lord Mayor’s Community Fund – Northgate\" in heldout rows 237/411). The same award re-published with a normalised dash or apostrophe yields a different `record_id` and a different `entity_ref`, so dedupe and cross-snapshot traceability break. Revise: normalise the key inputs (Unicode NFKC, dash/apostrophe folding, case-fold) before hashing, and document the normalisation in `record_id.rationale`.",
    "`dyn.grant_programme.code` is derived from `grant_list` with regex `^([^,]+),` (mapping field for `dyn.grant_programme.code`), but `dyn.grant_programme.name` is derived from the separate `grant` field. The two are not verified to agree. If `grant_list` label and `grant` label diverge (dash style already differs between rows), the code is attached to a programme entity keyed on `grant` alone (`entities.grant_programme.key_paths` = [\"grant\"]), so a code from one label can be bound to a programme identified by a different label. Revise: either derive both name and code from the same field, or add a validation that the `grant_list` label equals `grant` and refuse/flag mismatches.",
    "`dyn.grant_programme` is keyed on the full `grant` string, which includes ward/locality suffixes (\"Lord Mayor's Community Fund - Coorparoo\", \"- Bracken Ridge\", \"- Northgate\", \"- Moorooka\", \"- McDowall\"). This makes each ward a distinct programme entity, so `AWARDS_GRANT` links awards to ward-scoped pseudo-programmes rather than the actual scheme. The concept definition says \"a named funding programme or scheme\"; a ward suffix is a delivery location, not a distinct scheme. Revise: either state explicitly that the publisher treats each ward variant as a separate programme (and justify), or split the ward/locality into a separate attribute and key the programme on the scheme name.",
    "`dyn.grant_award.recipient_name_as_published` is marked `personal_data: \"always\"` and emitted with `privacy: \"personal\"` for organisation recipients too (heldout rows 237, 319, 411 are incorporated associations/clubs). Marking organisation names as always-personal overstates privacy risk and will over-redact legitimate organisation matching evidence. Revise: set `personal_data` to a conditional/`sometimes` posture (or document that the field may contain personal data but is not always personal), consistent with the definition text that it \"may be a natural person or an organisation\".",
    "`dyn.grant_award.recipient_abn` is emitted for 85/111 records with `error_rate_of_present: 0.0` after an `abn` transform, but the validation does not show how many ABNs failed checksum and were dropped versus how many were absent. The `null_if` step only nulls literal \"\", \"NULL\", \"null\"; a checksum-invalid ABN would either be dropped silently or emitted. Revise: report checksum-failure counts separately from absence, and confirm invalid ABNs are nulled (not emitted) with an explicit unmapped/refusal reason.",
    "`dyn.grant_award.amount_approved` sets `qualifiers.currency` to a constant \"AUD\" with `source_path: null`. The dataset metadata does not state currency; AUD is inferred from the Brisbane City Council publisher. This is a reasonable inference but is an unsourced assumption. Revise: record the currency inference basis in the field rationale (publisher jurisdiction) and flag it as inferred, not source-stated.",
    "`observed_at` uses `fallback: \"resource_last_modified\"` with `source_path: null`, but the emitted observations show `observed_at` = the retrieval timestamp (2026-11-22T22:37:19+00:00), identical to `ingested_at`. That conflates \"when the source stated it\" with \"when you read it\", which the ontology explicitly separates (`observation.observed_at` vs `observation.ingested_at`). Revise: either populate `observed_at` from an actual source-stated date (none exists here, so it should be null or documented as unavailable) or state clearly that the fallback is the snapshot retrieval time and that no source-stated observation date exists.",
    "`RECEIVES_GRANT` is declared in the ontology but not emitted (validation relationships only show `AWARDS_GRANT: 111`). This is consistent with the stated policy of not resolving recipients, but the proposal should explicitly state that `RECEIVES_GRANT` is intentionally unpopulated in this pass and that `ext.entity.holder_kind` is likewise not emitted, so downstream consumers do not assume absence means no recipient."
  ],
  "semantic_risks": [
    "Ward-suffixed programme names may be mistaken for distinct funding schemes rather than locality variants of one scheme, distorting programme-level aggregation.",
    "Keying awards on raw `grant` text with inconsistent dash/apostrophe characters risks duplicate award entities across snapshots and broken `entity_ref` stability.",
    "Marking all recipient names as personal data may cause over-redaction of organisation recipients and loss of legitimate matching evidence.",
    "Inferred AUD currency is not source-stated; if the publisher ever publishes non-AUD amounts this constant would silently mislabel them.",
    "`observed_at` equal to `ingested_at` may be read as a source-stated observation date when it is only the retrieval time.",
    "ABN checksum failures are not separately reported, so the 85 emitted ABNs' validity is asserted but not evidenced in the validation summary."
  ],
  "checked_reuse": [
    "`entity.abn` reuse: correctly deferred; award-level `dyn.grant_award.recipient_abn` is kept separate because recipient identity is unresolved and ABN presence does not establish company status. Reuse is conditional and documented.",
    "`ext.entity.holder_kind` reuse: proposed as matching evidence only, explicitly not implying sole_trader or legal form. Appropriate, though not emitted in this pass.",
    "`ext.financial.total_income` / `taxable_income` / `tax_payable` / `revenue_band`: correctly NOT reused for `amount_approved`; award amount is a third-party funding decision, not recipient income. Good.",
    "`ext.entity.sector_tag` / `ext.entity.industry_code`: correctly NOT reused for `project` or `group`; those are award text and publisher programme taxonomy, not business-owned classifications. Good.",
    "`ext.establishment.program`: correctly distinguished from `dyn.grant_programme`; site-delivered programme code is a different owner and meaning. Good.",
    "`observation.source_record_id`: correctly notes `index` is not durable and constructs a deterministic id; however the construction inputs need normalisation (see findings).",
    "`observation.licence`: CC-BY-4.0 recorded from metadata; consistent with `licence_detail` in emitted observations. Good."
  ],
  "rationale": "The proposal correctly separates grant awards and programmes from business entities, avoids treating award amounts as income, avoids inferring legal form from ABNs or personal names, and documents unmapped `index` and `grant_list`. Validation shows full coverage with zero field errors. However, the record identity is built on raw `grant` text that demonstrably varies in dash/apostrophe style across heldout rows, threatening cross-snapshot stability; the programme entity is keyed on ward-suffixed labels, conflating locality with scheme; the programme code is derived from a different field than the programme name without a consistency check; recipient names are over-marked as always-personal; ABN checksum-failure handling is not evidenced; currency is an unsourced constant; and `observed_at` is conflated with `ingested_at`. These are bounded, actionable semantic issues rather than fatal errors, so the verdict is revise, not reject. This is an agent review, not human sign-off."

      "Record identity is not stable across the snapshot: `mapping.record_id.source_paths` = [\"grant\", \"grant_round\", \"organisation_recipient\", \"project\", \"amount_approved\"] and `entities.grant_award.key_paths` mirror this, but `grant` embeds ward/locality text that varies in punctuation and dash style (e.g. \"Lord Mayor's Community Fund - Coorparoo\" vs \"Lord Mayor’s Community Fund – Northgate\" in heldout rows 237/411). The same award re-published with a normalised dash or apostrophe yields a different `record_id` and a different `entity_ref`, so dedupe and cross-snapshot traceability break. Revise: normalise the key inputs (Unicode NFKC, dash/apostrophe folding, case-fold) before hashing, and document the normalisation in `record_id.rationale`.",
      "`dyn.grant_programme.code` is derived from `grant_list` with regex `^([^,]+),` (mapping field for `dyn.grant_programme.code`), but `dyn.grant_programme.name` is derived from the separate `grant` field. The two are not verified to agree. If `grant_list` label and `grant` label diverge (dash style already differs between rows), the code is attached to a programme entity keyed on `grant` alone (`entities.grant_programme.key_paths` = [\"grant\"]), so a code from one label can be bound to a programme identified by a different label. Revise: either derive both name and code from the same field, or add a validation that the `grant_list` label equals `grant` and refuse/flag mismatches.",
      "`dyn.grant_programme` is keyed on the full `grant` string, which includes ward/locality suffixes (\"Lord Mayor's Community Fund - Coorparoo\", \"- Bracken Ridge\", \"- Northgate\", \"- Moorooka\", \"- McDowall\"). This makes each ward a distinct programme entity, so `AWARDS_GRANT` links awards to ward-scoped pseudo-programmes rather than the actual scheme. The concept definition says \"a named funding programme or scheme\"; a ward suffix is a delivery location, not a distinct scheme. Revise: either state explicitly that the publisher treats each ward variant as a separate programme (and justify), or split the ward/locality into a separate attribute and key the programme on the scheme name.",
      "`dyn.grant_award.recipient_name_as_published` is marked `personal_data: \"always\"` and emitted with `privacy: \"personal\"` for organisation recipients too (heldout rows 237, 319, 411 are incorporated associations/clubs). Marking organisation names as always-personal overstates privacy risk and will over-redact legitimate organisation matching evidence. Revise: set `personal_data` to a conditional/`sometimes` posture (or document that the field may contain personal data but is not always personal), consistent with the definition text that it \"may be a natural person or an organisation\".",
      "`dyn.grant_award.recipient_abn` is emitted for 85/111 records with `error_rate_of_present: 0.0` after an `abn` transform, but the validation does not show how many ABNs failed checksum and were dropped versus how many were absent. The `null_if` step only nulls literal \"\", \"NULL\", \"null\"; a checksum-invalid ABN would either be dropped silently or emitted. Revise: report checksum-failure counts separately from absence, and confirm invalid ABNs are nulled (not emitted) with an explicit unmapped/refusal reason.",
      "`dyn.grant_award.amount_approved` sets `qualifiers.currency` to a constant \"AUD\" with `source_path: null`. The dataset metadata does not state currency; AUD is inferred from the Brisbane City Council publisher. This is a reasonable inference but is an unsourced assumption. Revise: record the currency inference basis in the field rationale (publisher jurisdiction) and flag it as inferred, not source-stated.",
      "`observed_at` uses `fallback: \"resource_last_modified\"` with `source_path: null`, but the emitted observations show `observed_at` = the retrieval timestamp (2026-11-22T22:37:19+00:00), identical to `ingested_at`. That conflates \"when the source stated it\" with \"when you read it\", which the ontology explicitly separates (`observation.observed_at` vs `observation.ingested_at`). Revise: either populate `observed_at` from an actual source-stated date (none exists here, so it should be null or documented as unavailable) or state clearly that the fallback is the snapshot retrieval time and that no source-stated observation date exists.",
      "`RECEIVES_GRANT` is declared in the ontology but not emitted (validation relationships only show `AWARDS_GRANT: 111`). This is consistent with the stated policy of not resolving recipients, but the proposal should explicitly state that `RECEIVES_GRANT` is intentionally unpopulated in this pass and that `ext.entity.holder_kind` is likewise not emitted, so downstream consumers do not assume absence means no recipient."
    ],
    "semantic_risks": [
      "Ward-suffixed programme names may be mistaken for distinct funding schemes rather than locality variants of one scheme, distorting programme-level aggregation.",
      "Keying awards on raw `grant` text with inconsistent dash/apostrophe characters risks duplicate award entities across snapshots and broken `entity_ref` stability.",
      "Marking all recipient names as personal data may cause over-redaction of organisation recipients and loss of legitimate matching evidence.",
      "Inferred AUD currency is not source-stated; if the publisher ever publishes non-AUD amounts this constant would silently mislabel them.",
      "`observed_at` equal to `ingested_at` may be read as a source-stated observation date when it is only the retrieval time.",
      "ABN checksum failures are not separately reported, so the 85 emitted ABNs' validity is asserted but not evidenced in the validation summary."
    ],
    "checked_reuse": [
      "`entity.abn` reuse: correctly deferred; award-level `dyn.grant_award.recipient_abn` is kept separate because recipient identity is unresolved and ABN presence does not establish company status. Reuse is conditional and documented.",
      "`ext.entity.holder_kind` reuse: proposed as matching evidence only, explicitly not implying sole_trader or legal form. Appropriate, though not emitted in this pass.",
      "`ext.financial.total_income` / `taxable_income` / `tax_payable` / `revenue_band`: correctly NOT reused for `amount_approved`; award amount is a third-party funding decision, not recipient income. Good.",
      "`ext.entity.sector_tag` / `ext.entity.industry_code`: correctly NOT reused for `project` or `group`; those are award text and publisher programme taxonomy, not business-owned classifications. Good.",
      "`ext.establishment.program`: correctly distinguished from `dyn.grant_programme`; site-delivered programme code is a different owner and meaning. Good.",
      "`observation.source_record_id`: correctly notes `index` is not durable and constructs a deterministic id; however the construction inputs need normalisation (see findings).",
      "`observation.licence`: CC-BY-4.0 recorded from metadata; consistent with `licence_detail` in emitted observations. Good."
    ],
    "rationale": "The proposal correctly separates grant awards and programmes from business entities, avoids treating award amounts as income, avoids inferring legal form from ABNs or personal names, and documents unmapped `index` and `grant_list`. Validation shows full coverage with zero field errors. However, the record identity is built on raw `grant` text that demonstrably varies in dash/apostrophe style across heldout rows, threatening cross-snapshot stability; the programme entity is keyed on ward-suffixed labels, conflating locality with scheme; the programme code is derived from a different field than the programme name without a consistency check; recipient names are over-marked as always-personal; ABN checksum-failure handling is not evidenced; currency is an unsourced constant; and `observed_at` is conflated with `ingested_at`. These are bounded, actionable semantic issues rather than fatal errors, so the verdict is revise, not reject. This is an agent review, not human sign-off."
  }
}

{
  "verdict": "revise",
  "findings": [
    "dyn.grant_programme.name (mapping.fields[5], source_paths ['grant']) emits the ward-scoped label verbatim (e.g. 'Lord Mayor's Community Fund - Coorparoo', 'Lord Mayor’s Community Fund – Northgate' in emitted_examples). The concept definition states the suffix is a delivery-location variant and that the programme should be keyed on the scheme name, yet the emitted name retains the suffix and the entity key (mapping.entities.grant_programme.key_paths ['grant']) is the ward-scoped string. This contradicts the stated intent and fragments one scheme into many programme entities. Either strip the suffix from name and key on the scheme name, or drop the locality_variant concept and treat the full label as the programme name — but not both.",
    "dyn.grant_programme.code (mapping.fields[6]) claims 'Only emitted when the grant_list label matches the grant field after Unicode NFKC, dash/apostrophe folding and case-folding; mismatches are flagged and the code is withheld.' The claim is unsupported by the mapping.",
    "dyn.grant_programme.locality_variant (mapping.fields[8]) uses regex '\\s+[-\\u2013\\u2014]\\s+(.+)$' on the grant label. This is an unsupported semantic assumption.",
    "dyn.grant_award.recipient_name_as_published is declared personal_data 'always' in the concept, but the definition text says 'May contain personal data when the recipient is a natural person, but is not always personal.'     "record_id strategy (mapping.record_id) hashes grant, grant_round, organisation_recipient, project, amount_approved.     "locality_variant asserts the suffix is a delivery location without source evidence; a label like 'Lord Mayor's Community Fund - Coorparoo' could be a distinct sub-programme, not merely a location. Treating it as a location is an unsupported semantic assumption.",
    "recipient_name_as_published personal_data 'always' conflicts with the definition and with organisational recipients; over-classification may cause unnecessary redaction of organisation names.",

    "observation.licence: CC-BY-4.0 correctly recorded from metadata.",

  "rationale": "The proposal correctly avoids the main traps: it does not treat grant amounts as business income, does not infer company status from ABN or personal names, keeps recipient identity unresolved, and does not reuse business-owned financial or sector concepts. These are semantic errors or unsupported assumptions, not merely bounded risks. Currency inference and observed_at fallback are acceptable if explicitly bounded. Verdict: revise."
